\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}
, we have that
\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}
is perpendicular to
\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}
and

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle^{2}\right)-\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle^{2}\right)=\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle^{2}.

Now, let us consider the quadratic form

\displaystyle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{N}}\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle{\bm{f}}_{j}^{\intercal}\right){\mathbf{N}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle{\bm{f}}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle^{2}\lambda_{j}\geq\lambda_{k+1}\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2},

(4.2)

where the last inequality follows by the fact that
\lambda_{i}\geq 0
holds for any
1\leq i\leq n
. This gives us that

\displaystyle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{N}}\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(u,v)\in{E}}{w}(u,v)\left(\frac{\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}(u)}{\sqrt{{d}(u)}}-\frac{\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}(v)}{\sqrt{{d}(v)}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(u,v)\in{E}}{w}(u,v)\left(\frac{{\bm{\chi}}_{i}(u)}{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}-\frac{{\bm{\chi}}_{i}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{{w}({S}_{i},\overline{{S}_{i}})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\leq\rho(k).

(4.3)

Combining (
4.2
) with (
4.3
), we have that

\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{N}}\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}}{\lambda_{k+1}}\leq\frac{\rho(k)}{\lambda_{k+1}}\leq\frac{1}{\Upsilon(k)},

which proves the first statement of the theorem.

Now we prove the second statement.
We define for any
1\leq i\leq k
that

\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle{\bm{f}}_{i},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}
and have that, since
{\bm{f}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{i}
is perpendicular to
\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{i}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left\|{\bm{f}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(\left\|{\bm{f}}_{i}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(1-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j},{\bm{f}}_{i}\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=k-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j},{\bm{f}}_{i}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left(1-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j},{\bm{f}}_{i}\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left(\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}-\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{1}{\Upsilon(k)}

\displaystyle=\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)},

where the inequality follows by the first statement of Theorem
4.1
.
∎

4.2
Analysis of Spectral Clustering

For any input graph
{G}=({V},{E})
and
k\in[n]
, we study the following spectral clustering algorithm:

1.

compute the eigenvectors
{\bm{f}}_{1},\ldots{\bm{f}}_{k}
of
{\mathbf{N}}
, and embed each
u\in{V}
to the point
F(u)\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
defined by

F(u)\triangleq\frac{1}{\sqrt{{d}(u)}}\left({\bm{f}}_{1}(u),\ldots,{\bm{f}}_{k}(u)\right)^{\intercal};

(4.4)

2.

apply
k
-means on the embedded points
\{F(u)\}_{u\in{V}}
;

3.

partition
{V}
into
k
clusters based on the output of
k
-means.

In this rest of this section, we prove the following theorem, where
APT
is the approximation ratio of the
k
-means algorithm used in spectral clustering.
Recall that we can take
APT
to be some small constant
[
CGKR21
,
KSS04
]
.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
{G}=({V},{E})
be a graph with
k
clusters
S_{1},\ldots,S_{k}
of almost-balanced size, and

\Upsilon(k)\geq 2176(1+\textsf{APT})
.
Let
\{{A}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
be the output of spectral clustering and assume, without loss of generality, that the optimal correspondent of
{A}_{i}
is
{S}_{i}
. Then, it holds that

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{vol}\left({A}_{i}\triangle{S}_{i}\right)\leq 2176(1+\textsf{APT})\frac{\mathrm{vol}({V})}{\Upsilon(k)}.

The most significant feature of Theorem
4.2
is that the lower bound of
\Upsilon(k)
is independent of the number of clusters
k
.
The theorem gives a non-trivial guarantee on the performance of spectral clustering so long as the spectral gap, as measured by
\Upsilon(k)
, is at least a constant.
This improves on the previous analysis of graph clustering algorithms which make stronger assumptions on the input graphs
[
PSZ17
,
Miz21
,
LOGT14
]
.

4.2.1
Properties of Spectral Embedding

Let us first study the properties of the spectral embedding defined in (
4.4
), and in the next subsection we use these properties to prove Theorem
4.2
.
For every cluster
{S}_{i}
, define the vector
{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
by

{\bm{p}}^{(i)}(j)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}\langle{\bm{f}}_{j},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\rangle,

and we can view these
\{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{k}
as the approximate centres of the embedded points from the optimal clusters
\{{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
.
We prove that the total
k
-means cost of the embedded points can be upper bounded as follows:

Lemma 4.1
.

It holds that

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}{d}(u)\left\|F(u)-{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}{d}(u)\left\|F(u)-{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}{d}(u)\left[\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left(\frac{{\bm{f}}_{j}(u)}{\sqrt{{d}(u)}}-\frac{\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle}{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left({\bm{f}}_{j}(u)-\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}(u)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left({\bm{f}}_{j}(u)-\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{j}(u)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left\|{\bm{f}}_{j}-\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)},

where we use that for
u\in{S}_{x}
it holds that
\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{i}(u)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle{\bm{f}}_{i},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}(u)=\langle{\bm{f}}_{i},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{x}\rangle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{x}(u)
, and the final inequality follows by the second statement of Theorem
4.1
.
∎

The importance of Lemma
4.1
is that, although the optimal centres for
k
-means are unknown, the existence of
\{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{k}
is sufficient to show that the cost of an optimal
k
-means clustering on
\{F(u)\}_{u\in{V}}
is at most
k/\Upsilon(k)
.
Since one can always use an
O(1)
-approximate
k
-means algorithm for spectral clustering (e.g.,
[
KMN+04
,
KSS04
]
), the cost of the output of
k
-means on
\{F(u)\}_{u\in{V}}
is
O\left(k/\Upsilon(k)\right)
.
Next, we show that the
length of
{\bm{p}}^{(i)}
is approximately equal to

1/\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})
,
which is used in our later analysis.

Lemma 4.2
.

It holds for any
i\in[k]
that

\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\left(1-\frac{1}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)\leq\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}.

Proof.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle{\bm{f}}_{j},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\rangle^{2}=\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}=1-\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\geq 1-\frac{1}{\Upsilon(k)},

where the inequality follows by Theorem
4.1
. The other direction of the inequality follows similarly.
∎

In the remainder of this subsection, we prove a sequence of lemmas showing that any pair of
{\bm{p}}^{(i)}
and
{\bm{p}}^{(j)}
for
i\neq j
are well separated.
Moreover, their distance is essentially independent of
k
and
\Upsilon(k)
, as long as
\Upsilon(k)\geq 20
.

Lemma 4.3
.

It holds for any different
i,j\in[k]
that

\left\|\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}\geq 2-\frac{8}{\Upsilon(k)}.

Proof.

By the definitions of
{\bm{p}}^{(i)}
and
\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{k}\left(\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\rangle-\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{x=1}^{k}\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\rangle^{2}\right)+\left(\sum_{x=1}^{k}\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle^{2}\right)-2\sum_{x=1}^{k}\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\rangle\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}\right\|^{2}-2\left\lvert\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}^{\intercal}\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}\right\rvert.

Then, considering only the final term, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}^{\intercal}\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert(\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}+\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i})^{\intercal}(\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}+\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j})\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle+\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j},\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle+\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j},\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle\right\rvert\leq\frac{3}{\Upsilon(k)},

where we use the fact that
\left\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\right\rangle=0
for
i\neq j
and
\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\leq 1/\Upsilon(k)
.
Then, since
\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\geq 1-1/\Upsilon(k)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2\left(1-\frac{1}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)-\frac{6}{\Upsilon(k)}

\displaystyle=2-\frac{8}{\Upsilon(k)}.\qed

Lemma 4.4
.

It holds for any different
i,j\in[k]
that

\left\|\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\|^{2}\geq 2-\frac{20}{\Upsilon(k)}.

Proof.

Assume without loss of generality that
\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|
.
Let
{\bm{a}}_{i}=\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}{\bm{p}}^{(i)}
and
{\bm{a}}_{j}=\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}{\bm{p}}^{(j)}
and notice that
\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|\leq\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|\leq 1
by Lemma
4.2
.
Then, since
{\bm{a}}_{i}
and
(\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|/\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|){\bm{a}}_{j}
are scaled versions of
{\bm{p}}^{(i)}
and
{\bm{p}}^{(j)}
with smaller norm, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}-\frac{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|}{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|}{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}-{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|-\left(\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|-\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{2-\frac{8}{\Upsilon(k)}}-\left(\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\cdot\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|-\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{2}\left(1-\frac{4}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)+\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{\Upsilon(k)}}-1

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{2}-\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\Upsilon(k)}-\frac{1}{\Upsilon(k)}\geq\sqrt{2}-\frac{7}{\Upsilon(k)},

where the second inequality follows by the triangle inequality, and the third and fourth use Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.2
. We also use the fact that it holds for
x\leq 1
that
\sqrt{1-x}\geq 1-x
.
This gives

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2-\frac{14\sqrt{2}}{\Upsilon(k)}\geq 2-\frac{20}{\Upsilon(k)},

which proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

It holds for any different
i,j\in[k]
that

\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{\min\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{8}{\Upsilon(k)}\right).

Proof.

Assume without loss of generality that
\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|\geq\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|
. Then, let
\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|=\alpha\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|
for some
\alpha\in[0,1]
.
By Lemma
4.2
, it holds that

\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{\min\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\}}\left(1-\frac{1}{\Upsilon(k)}\right).

Additionally, notice that by the proof of Lemma
4.4
,

\left\langle\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|},\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rangle\leq\sqrt{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}+\frac{7}{2\Upsilon(k)},

where we use the fact that if
x^{2}+y^{2}=1
, then
x+y\leq\sqrt{2}
.
One can understand the equation above by considering the right-angled triangle with one edge given by
{\bm{p}}^{(i)}/\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|
and another edge given by
({\bm{p}}^{(i)}/\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|).({\bm{p}}^{(j)}/\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|)
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}+\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}-2\left\langle\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|},\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rangle\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq(1+\alpha)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}-\left(\sqrt{2}+\frac{7}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)\alpha\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-(\sqrt{2}-1)\alpha-\frac{7}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\min\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{7}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\min\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{8}{\Upsilon(k)}\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

It is worth noting that, despite the similarity in their formulation, the technical lemmas presented here are stronger than the ones in Peng et al.
[
PSZ17
]
.
These results are obtained through the stronger structure theorem (Theorem
4.1
), and are crucial for us to prove Theorem
4.2
.

4.2.2
Bounding the Misclassified Vertices

Now we prove
Theorem
4.2
,
and see why a mild condition like

\Upsilon(k)\geq C
for some constant
C\in\mathbb{N}

suffices for spectral clustering to perform well in practice.
Let
\{{A}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
be the output of spectral clustering, and we denote the centre of the embedded points
\{F(u)\}_{u\in{A}_{i}}
for any
{A}_{i}
by
{\bm{c}}_{i}
.
As the starting point of the analysis, we show that every
{\bm{c}}_{i}
is close to its ‘optimal’ correspondent
{\bm{p}}^{(\sigma(i))}
for some
\sigma(i)\in[k]
. That is, the actual centre of embedded points from every
{A}_{i}
is close to the approximate centre of the embedded points from some optimal
{S}_{i}
.
To formalise this, we define the function
\sigma:[k]\rightarrow[k]
by

\sigma(i)=\argmin_{j\in[k]}\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-{\bm{c}}_{i}\right\|;

(4.5)

that is, cluster
{A}_{i}
should correspond to
{S}_{\sigma(i)}
in which the value of
\|{\bm{p}}^{(\sigma(i))}-{\bm{c}}_{i}\|
is the lowest among all the distances between
{\bm{c}}_{i}
and all of the
{\bm{p}}^{(j)}
for
j\in[k]
.
However, one needs to be cautious as (
4.5
) doesn’t necessarily define a permutation, and there might exist different
i,i^{\prime}\in[k]
such that both of
{A}_{i}
and
{A}_{i^{\prime}}
map to the same
{S}_{\sigma(i)}
.
Taking this into account, for any fixed
\sigma:[k]\rightarrow[k]
and
i\in[k]
, we further define
{M}_{\sigma,i}
by

{M}_{\sigma,i}\triangleq\bigcup_{j:\sigma(j)=i}{A}_{j}.

(4.6)

The following lemma shows that, when mapping every output
{A}_{i}
to
{S}_{\sigma(i)}
, the total ratio of misclassified volume with respect to each cluster can be upper bounded:

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\{{A}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
be the output of spectral clustering, and
\sigma
and
{M}_{\sigma,i}
be defined as in (
4.5
) and (
4.6
).
If
\Upsilon(k)\geq 32
, then

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\leq 64(1+\textsf{APT})\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

Proof.

Let us define
{B}_{ij}={A}_{i}\cap{S}_{j}
to be the vertices in
{A}_{i}
which belong to the ground-truth cluster
{S}_{j}
.
Then, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}^{k}\mathrm{vol}({B}_{ij})\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{\sigma(i)})}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({B}_{ij})}{\min\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{\sigma(i)}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\}},

(4.7)

and that

\displaystyle\mathrm{COST}({A}_{1},\ldots{A}_{k})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{A}_{i}}{d}(u)\left\|F(u)-{\bm{c}}_{i}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in{B}_{ij}}{d}(u)\left\|F(u)-{\bm{c}}_{i}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in{B}_{ij}}{d}(u)\left(\frac{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-{\bm{c}}_{i}\right\|^{2}}{2}-\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-F(u)\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in{B}_{ij}}\frac{{d}(u)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-{\bm{p}}^{(\sigma(i))}\right\|^{2}}{8}-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in{B}_{ij}}{d}(u)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-F(u)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\mathrm{vol}({B}_{ij})\frac{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-{\bm{p}}^{(\sigma(i))}\right\|^{2}}{8}-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}{d}(u)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-F(u)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({B}_{ij})}{8\min\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{\sigma(i)}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{8}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}{d}(u)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-F(u)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{16}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{8}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)-\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)},

where the second inequality follows by the inequality

\left\|{\bm{a}}-{\bm{b}}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{\left\|{\bm{b}}-{\bm{c}}\right\|^{2}}{2}-\left\|{\bm{a}}-{\bm{c}}\right\|^{2},

the third inequality follows since
{\bm{c}}_{i}
is closer to
{\bm{p}}^{(\sigma(i))}
than
{\bm{p}}^{(j)}
, the fifth one follows from Lemma
4.5
, and the last one follows by (
4.7
).

On the other side, since
\mathrm{COST}({A}_{1},\ldots,{A}_{k})\leq\textsf{APT}\cdot k/\Upsilon(k)
, we have that

\frac{1}{16}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{8}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)-\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}\leq\textsf{APT}\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}

\displaystyle\leq 16\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{8}{\Upsilon(k)}\right)^{-1}\leq 64\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)},

where the last inequality holds by the assumption that
\Upsilon(k)\geq 32
.
∎

It remains to study the case in which
\sigma
isn’t a permutation. Notice that, if this occurs, there is some
i\in[k]
such that
{M}_{\sigma,i}=\emptyset
, and different values of
x,y\in[k]
such that
\sigma(x)=\sigma(y)=j
for some
j\neq i
.
Based on this, we construct the function
\sigma^{\prime}:[k]\rightarrow[k]
from
\sigma
based on the following procedure:

•

set
\sigma^{\prime}(z)=i
if
z=x
;

•

set
\sigma^{\prime}(z)=\sigma(z)
for any other
z\in[k]\setminus\{x\}
.

Notice that one can construct
\sigma^{\prime}
in this way as long as
\sigma
isn’t a permutation, and this constructed
\sigma^{\prime}
reduces the number of
{M}_{\sigma,i}
which are equal to
\emptyset
by one.
We show that one only needs to construct such
\sigma^{\prime}
at most
64(1+\textsf{APT})(k/\Upsilon(k))
times
to obtain the final permutation called
\sigma^{\star}
, and it holds for
\sigma^{\star}
that

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\star},i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\leq 1088\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

Combining this with the fact that
\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})=\Theta(\mathrm{vol}({V}_{G})/k)
for any
i\in[k]
proves Theorem
4.2
.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

By Lemma
4.1
, we have

\mathsf{COST}({S}_{1},\ldots,{S}_{k})\leq\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

Combining this with the fact that one can apply an approximate
k
-means clustering algorithm with approximation ratio
APT
for spectral clustering, we have that

\mathsf{COST}({A}_{1},\ldots,{A}_{k})\leq\textsf{APT}\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

Then, let
\sigma:[1,k]\rightarrow[1,k]
be the function which assigns each cluster
{A}_{i}
to some ground-truth cluster
{S}_{\sigma(i)}
such that

\sigma(i)=\argmin_{j\in[k]}\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-{\bm{c}}_{i}\right\|
.
Then, it holds by Lemma
4.6
that

\epsilon(\sigma)\triangleq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\leq 64\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

(4.8)

Now, assume that
\sigma
is not a permutation, and we’ll apply the following procedure inductively to construct a permutation from
\sigma
. Since
\sigma
isn’t a permutation, there is
i\in[k]
such that
M_{\sigma,i}=\emptyset
. Therefore, there are different values of
x,y\in[k]
such that
\sigma(x)=\sigma(y)=j
for some
j\neq i
. Based on this, we construct the function
\sigma^{\prime}:[k]\rightarrow[k]
such that
\sigma^{\prime}(z)=i
if
z=x
, and
\sigma^{\prime}(z)=\sigma(z)
for any other
z\in[k]\setminus\{x\}
. Notice that we can construct
\sigma^{\prime}
in this way as long as
\sigma
isn’t a permutation.
By the definition of
\epsilon(\cdot)
and function
\sigma^{\prime}
, the difference between
\epsilon(\sigma^{\prime})
and
\epsilon(\sigma)
can be written as

\displaystyle\epsilon(\sigma^{\prime})-\epsilon(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left(\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\prime},i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}-\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\right)}_{\alpha}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\prime},j}\triangle{S}_{j})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}-\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,j}\triangle{S}_{j})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\right)}_{\beta}.

(4.9)

Let us consider
4
cases based on the sign of
\alpha,\beta
defined above.
In each case, we bound the cost introduced by the change from
\sigma
to
\sigma^{\prime}
, and then consider the total cost introduced throughout the entire procedure of constructing a permutation.

Case 1:
\alpha<0,\beta<0
.

In this case, it is clear that
\epsilon(\sigma^{\prime})-\epsilon(\sigma)\leq 0
, and hence the total introduced cost
is at most
0
.

Case 2:
\alpha>0,\beta<0
.

In this case, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon(\sigma^{\prime})-\epsilon(\sigma)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{multlined}\frac{1}{\min(\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j}))}\Big(\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\prime},i}\triangle{S}_{i})-\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})\qquad\\
+\left\lvert\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\prime},j}\triangle{S}_{j})-\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,j}\triangle{S}_{j})\right\rvert\Big)\end{multlined}

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}\frac{1}{\min(\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j}))}\Big(\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\prime},i}\triangle{S}_{i})-\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})\qquad\\
+\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,j}\triangle{S}_{j})-\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\prime},j}\triangle{S}_{j})\Big)\end{multlined}

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}\frac{1}{\min(\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j}))}\big(\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\setminus{S}_{i})-\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\cap{S}_{i})\qquad\\
+\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\setminus{S}_{j})-\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\cap{S}_{j})\big)\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\cdot\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\setminus{S}_{j})}{\min(\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\cdot\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\setminus{S}_{j})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})},

where the last inequality follows by the fact that the clusters are almost balanced.
Since each set
{A}_{x}
is moved at most once in order to construct a permutation, the total introduced cost due to this case is at most

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{{A}_{x}\in{M}_{\sigma,j}}\frac{8\cdot\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\setminus{S}_{j})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\leq 8\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,j}\triangle{S}_{j})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\leq 512\cdot\left(1+\textsf{APT}\right)\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

Case 3:
\alpha>0,\beta>0
.

In this case, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon(\sigma^{\prime})-\epsilon(\sigma)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{multlined}\frac{1}{\min(\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j}))}\big(\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\prime},i}\triangle{S}_{i})-\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})\qquad\\
+\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\prime},j}\triangle{S}_{j})-\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,j}\triangle{S}_{j})\big)\end{multlined}

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}\frac{1}{\min(\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j}))}\big(\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\setminus{S}_{i})-\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\cap{S}_{i})\qquad\\
+\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\cap{S}_{j})-\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\setminus{S}_{j})\big)\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\min(\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j}))}\left(2\cdot\mathrm{vol}({S}_{x}\cap{S}_{j})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\cdot\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}{\min(\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j}))}

\displaystyle\leq 8,

where the last inequality follows by the fact that the clusters are almost balanced.
We will consider the total introduced cost due to this case and Case 4 together, and so let’s first examine Case 4.

Case 4:
\alpha<0,\beta>0
.

In this case, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon(\sigma^{\prime})-\epsilon(\sigma)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\left(\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\prime},j}\triangle{S}_{j})-\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,j}\triangle{S}_{j})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\left(\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\cap{S}_{j})-\mathrm{vol}({A}_{x}\setminus{S}_{j})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}

\displaystyle=1.

Now, let us bound the total number of times we need to construct
\sigma^{\prime}
in order to obtain a permutation.
For any
i
with
{M}_{\sigma,i}=\emptyset
, we have

\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}=\frac{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}=1,

so the total number of required iterations is upper bounded by

\left\lvert\{i:{M}_{\sigma,i}=\emptyset\}\right\rvert\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\leq 64\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

As such, the total introduced cost due to Cases 3 and 4 is at most

8\cdot 64\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}=512\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

Putting everything together, we have that

\epsilon(\sigma^{\star})\leq\epsilon(\sigma)+1024\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}\leq 1088\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\frac{k}{\Upsilon(k)}.

This implies that

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma^{\star},i}\triangle{S}_{i})\leq 2176\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\frac{\mathrm{vol}({V}_{G})}{\Upsilon(k)},

and completes the proof.
∎

It is worth noting that this method of upper bounding the ratio of misclassified vertices is very different from the ones used in previous references, e.g.,
[
DPRS19
,
Miz21
,
PSZ17
]
. In particular, instead of examining all the possible mappings between
\{{A}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
and
\{{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
,
in this proof we directly work with some specifically defined function
\sigma
, and constructed our desired mapping
\sigma^{\star}
from
\sigma
. This is another key for us to obtain stronger results than the previous work.

4.3
Future Work

Notice that the analysis presented in this chapter requires that the optimal clusters have almost-balanced size, which is the only technical assumption of our result. This raises a natural question on the necessity of such a condition.

Open Question 1
.

Is it possible for spectral clustering to theoretically achieve the same approximation guarantee as Theorem
4.2
when the optimal clusters of an input graph have arbitrary sizes? Alternatively, is there a hard instance showing that our presented analysis is tight up to some constant factor?

Secondly, it would be very interesting to see whether
one can theoretically analyse the performance of spectral clustering with respect to
\lambda_{k+1}/\lambda_{k}
, instead of
\Upsilon(k)
.

Open Question 2
.

Is it possible to upper bound the number (or volume) of misclassified vertices from spectral clustering with respect to the ratio between
\lambda_{k+1}
and
\lambda_{k}
?

Notice that, in contrast to
\Upsilon(k)
, the value of
\lambda_{k+1}/\lambda_{k}
can be computed in polynomial time; therefore, an affirmative answer to the question will have more practical impact than our current presented result.
In addition, such an answer would further justify the eigen-gap heuristic
[
vL07
]
, helping to close
the gap between the theoretical analysis of spectral clustering and its empirical performance.

Chapter 5Spectral Clustering with Meta-Graphs

In this chapter we build on the analysis of the last chapter and develop a variant of spectral clustering which employs fewer than
k
eigenvectors to find
k
clusters.
We prove the surprising result that,
when the structure among the optimal clusters in an input graph satisfies certain conditions,
spectral clustering with fewer eigenvectors is able to produce better results than classical spectral clustering.
This presents the opportunity to significantly speed up the runtime of spectral clustering in practice, since fewer eigenvectors are used to construct the embedding.

The starting point of our new analysis is the following observation: in many graphs arising from real-world data, the structure of clusters is not symmetric.
For example, let us consider the well-known MNIST dataset of handwritten digits
[
Hul94
]
.
This dataset consists of 70,000 images of handwritten digits 0 to 9.
Each image has
28\times 28
pixels, and each pixel has a greyscale value between 0 and 255.
Figure
5.1
shows some example images from the MNIST dataset.

Figure 5.1
:
Example images from the MNIST dataset.

Then, we can represent each image in the dataset by a vector
{\bm{x}}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{28\times 28}
and construct the
\bm{k}
nearest neighbours graph
,
{G}=({V},{E})
, in the following way:

•

for each data point
{\bm{x}}_{i}
in the dataset, there is a corresponding vertex
v_{i}\in{V}
;

•

for each
v_{i}
there is an undirected edge to the
k
vertices representing the data points with the smallest distances to
{\bm{x}}_{i}
.

Figure
5.2
illustrates the
k
nearest neighbour graph for a randomly chosen subset of the data.

Figure 5.2
:
The
k
nearest neighbour graph of a random subset of the MNIST dataset, for
k=3
. The graph has a cluster corresponding to each digit.

Notice that some of the clusters in this graph are more similar than others.
For example, the cluster representing the digit
4
has more connections with the cluster representing
9
than the one representing
6
.
The central result of this chapter is that this
asymmetry
can be used to improve the performance of spectral clustering, and allow us to recover all
10
clusters while using fewer than
10
eigenvectors of the graph Laplacian.

Main Result 3

(See Theorem
5.2
for the formal statement)
.

Given a graph
{G}
, let
{S}_{1},\ldots,{S}_{k}
be the clusters which achieve
{\rho}(k)
and suppose that
{S}_{1},\ldots,{S}_{k}
are almost-balanced.
Additionally, suppose that the clusters
{S}_{1},\ldots,{S}_{k}
exhibit some significant high-level structure.
Then, spectral clustering with fewer than
k
eigenvectors performs better than spectral clustering with
k
eigenvectors.

The significance of this result is further demonstrated by experiments on the BSDS image segmentation dataset.
It is well known that spectral methods are an effective technique for image segmentation
[
SM00
,
TWC10
,
ZZL+21
]
, and in this chapter we see that spectral clustering is effective for image segmentation, and that using fewer eigenvectors often results in a better segmentation.
Figure
5.3
shows examples in which, in order to find
6
and
45
clusters, spectral clustering with
3
and
7
eigenvectors produce better results than the ones with
6
and
45
eigenvectors.
Section
5.3
describes the full experimental details.

(a)

Original Image

(b)

6
clusters with
3
vectors

(c)

6
clusters with
6
vectors

(d)

Original Image

(e)

45
clusters with
7
vectors

(f)

45
clusters with
45
vectors

Figure 5.3
:
Examples of image segmentation using spectral clustering; the original images are from the BSDS dataset. The Rand Index of segmentation (b) is
0.83
, while (c) has Rand Index
0.78
. Segmentation (e) has Rand Index
0.92
, and (f) has Rand Index
0.80
. Hence, it’s clear that spectral clustering with fewer than
k
eigenvectors suffices to produce comparable or better output.

The result in this chapter is related to previous work on
efficient spectral algorithms to find cluster-structures.
While it’s known that flow and path structures of clusters in directed graphs can be uncovered with complex-valued Hermitian matrices
[
CLSZ20
,
LS20
]
, our result shows that one can apply real-valued Laplacians of undirected graphs, and find more general patterns of clusters.

5.1
Encoding the Cluster-Structure into Meta-Graphs

Suppose that
\{{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
is a partition of
{V}_{G}
that corresponds to the
k
-way expansion
\rho(k)
for an input graph
{G}
. We define the matrix
{\mathbf{A}}_{{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
by

{\mathbf{A}}_{M}(i,j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{w}({S}_{i},{S}_{j})&\mbox{if }i\neq j,\\
2{w}({S}_{i},{S}_{j})&\mbox{if }i=j\end{array}\right.

and, taking
{\mathbf{A}}_{M}
to be the adjacency matrix, this
defines a graph
{M}=({V}_{M},{E}_{M},{w}_{M})
which we refer to as the
meta-graph
of the clusters.
Informally, the meta-graph is constructed by viewing every cluster
{S}_{i}
as a ‘giant vertex’, and captures the structure of the optimal clusters.
Then, define the normalised adjacency matrix of
{M}
by

{\mathbf{B}}_{M}\triangleq{\mathbf{D}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{M}{\mathbf{A}}_{M}{\mathbf{D}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{M}
,
and the normalised Laplacian matrix of
{M}
by
{\mathbf{N}}_{M}\triangleq{\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{B}}_{M}
.
Let the eigenvalues of
{\mathbf{N}}_{M}
be
\gamma_{1}\leq\ldots\leq\gamma_{k}
, and
{\bm{g}}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
be the eigenvector corresponding to
\gamma_{i}
for any
i\in[k]
.

The starting point of our novel approach is to look at the structure of this meta-graph
{M}
, and study how the spectral information of
{\mathbf{N}}_{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
is encoded in the bottom eigenvectors of
{\mathbf{N}}_{G}
.
To achieve this,
for any
\ell\in[k]
and vertex
i\in{V}_{M}
, let

\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\triangleq\left({\bm{g}}_{1}(i),\ldots,{\bm{g}}_{\ell}(i)\right)^{\intercal};

(5.1)

notice that
\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
defines the spectral embedding of
i\in{V}_{M}
with the bottom
\ell
eigenvectors of
{\mathbf{N}}_{M}
.

Definition 5.1

(
(\theta,\ell)
-distinguishable graph)
.

For any
{M}=({V}_{M},{E}_{M},{w}_{M})
with
k
vertices,
\ell\in[k]
, and
\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, we say that
{M}
is
(\theta,\ell)
-distinguishable if

•

it holds for any
i\in[k]
that
\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\geq\theta
, and

•

it holds for any different
i,j\in[k]
that
\left\|\frac{\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\|^{2}\geq\theta
.

In other words, graph
{M}
is
(\theta,\ell)
-distinguishable if (i) every embedded point
\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}
has squared length at least
\theta
, and (ii) any pair of different embedded points with normalisation are separated by
a distance
of at least
\theta
.
By definition, it is easy to see that, if
{M}
is
(\theta,\ell)
-distinguishable for some large value of
\theta
, then the embedded points
\{\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\}_{i\in{V}_{M}}
can be easily separated even if
\ell<k
.
Examples
5.1
and
5.2
below demonstrate that this is indeed the case and, since the meta-graph
{M}
is constructed from
\{{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
, this well-separation property for
\{\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\}_{i\in{V}_{M}}
usually implies
that the clusters
\{{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
are also well separated when the vertices are mapped to the points
\{F(u)\}_{u\in{V}_{G}}
, in which

F(u)\triangleq\frac{1}{\sqrt{{d}(u)}}\cdot\left({\bm{f}}_{1}(u),\ldots{\bm{f}}_{\ell}(u)\right)^{\intercal}.

(5.2)

Example 5.1
.

Suppose the meta-graph is
C_{6}
, the cycle on
6
vertices.
Figure
5.4
(
5.4(a)
) shows that the vertices of
C_{6}
are well separated by the second and third eigenvectors of
{\mathbf{N}}_{C_{6}}
.
1
1

1

Notice that the first eigenvector is always the constant vector and gives no useful information. This is why we visualise the second and third eigenvectors only.

Since the minimum distance between any pair of vertices in this embedding is
2/3
, we say that
C_{6}
is
\left(2/3,3\right)
-distinguishable.
Figure
5.4
(
5.4(b)
) shows that, when using
{\bm{f}}_{2},{\bm{f}}_{3}
of
{\mathbf{N}}_{G}
to embed the vertices of a
600
-vertex graph with a cyclical cluster pattern, the embedded points closely match the ones from the meta-graph.
2
2

2

In Examples
5.1
and
5.2
, the graphs
G
are generated according to a generalised stochastic block model based on the defined meta-graph. The model is formally described in Section
5.3
.

(a)

Spectral embedding of
C_{6}
with
{\bm{g}}_{2}
and
{\bm{g}}_{3}
.

(b)

Spectral embedding of
{G}
with
{\bm{f}}_{2}
and
{\bm{f}}_{3}
.

Figure 5.4
:
The spectral embedding of a large graph
{G}
whose clusters exhibit a cyclical structure closely matches the embedding of the meta-graph
C_{6}
.

Example 5.2
.

Suppose the meta-graph is
P_{4,4}
, which is the
4\times 4
grid graph.
Figure
5.5
(
5.5(a)
) shows that the vertices are well separated using the second and third eigenvectors of
{\mathbf{N}}_{P_{4,4}}
.
The minimum distance between any pair of vertices in this embedding is roughly
0.1
, and so
P_{4,4}
is
(0.1,3)
-distinguishable.
Figure
5.5
(
5.5(b)
) demonstrates that,
when using
{\bm{f}}_{2},{\bm{f}}_{3}
of
{\mathbf{N}}_{G}
to construct the embedding, the embedded points closely match the ones from the meta-graph.

(a)

Embedding of
P_{4,4}
with
{\bm{g}}_{2}
and
{\bm{g}}_{3}
.

(b)

Embedding of
{G}
with
{\bm{f}}_{2}
and
{\bm{f}}_{3}
.

Figure 5.5
:

The spectral embedding of a large
1600
-vertex graph
{G}
whose clusters exhibit a grid structure closely matches the embedding of the meta-graph
P_{4,4}
.

From these examples, it is clear to see that there is a close
connection between the embedding
\{\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\}
defined in (
5.1
) and the embedding
\{F(u)\}
defined in (
5.2
). To formally analyse this connection, we develop a new structure theorem with meta-graphs.

We define vectors
\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
which represent the eigenvectors of
{\mathbf{N}}_{M}
‘blown-up’ to
n
dimensions.
Formally, we define
\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}
such that

\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{{\mathbf{D}}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{\chi}}_{j}}{\left\|{\mathbf{D}}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{\chi}}_{j}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{g}}_{i}(j),

where
{\bm{\chi}}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the indicator vector of cluster
{S}_{j}
defined in Section
2.3
.
By definition, it holds for any
u\in{S}_{j}
that

\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}(u)=\sqrt{\frac{{d}(u)}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}}\cdot{\bm{g}}_{i}(j).

The following lemma shows that
\{\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
form an orthonormal basis.

Lemma 5.1
.

The following statements hold:

1.

it holds for any
i\in[k]
that
\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|=1
;

2.

it holds for any different
i,j\in[k]
that
\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle=0
.

Proof.

By definition, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}=\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}^{\intercal}\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{j}}\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}(u)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{j}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{{d}(u)}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}}\cdot{\bm{g}}_{i}(j)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}{\bm{g}}_{i}(j)^{2}=\left\|{\bm{g}}_{i}\right\|^{2}=1,

which proves the first statement.

To prove the second statement, we have for any
i\neq j
that

\displaystyle\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{x}}\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}(u)\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{x}}\frac{{d}(u)}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{x})}\cdot{\bm{g}}_{i}(x){\bm{g}}_{j}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{k}{\bm{g}}_{i}(x){\bm{g}}_{j}(x)

\displaystyle={\bm{g}}_{i}^{\intercal}{\bm{g}}_{j}=0,

which completes the proof.
∎

The following lemma presents an
important relationship between the eigenvalues of
{\mathbf{N}}_{M}
and those of
{\mathbf{N}}_{G}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

It holds for any
i\in[k]
that
\lambda_{i}\leq\gamma_{i}
.

Proof.

Notice that we have for any
j\leq k
that

\displaystyle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{N}}_{G}\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(u,v)\in{E}_{G}}{w}_{G}(u,v)\left(\frac{\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}(u)}{\sqrt{{d}(u)}}-\frac{\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}(v)}{\sqrt{{d}(v)}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{k-1}\sum_{y=x+1}^{k}\sum_{a\in{S}_{x}}\sum_{b\in{S}_{y}}{w}(a,b)\left(\frac{\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}(a)}{\sqrt{{d}(a)}}-\frac{\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}(b)}{\sqrt{{d}(b)}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{k-1}\sum_{y=x+1}^{k}{w}({S}_{x},{S}_{y})\left(\frac{{\bm{g}}_{j}(x)}{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{x})}}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{j}(y)}{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{y})}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle={\bm{g}}_{j}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{N}}_{M}{\bm{g}}_{j}=\gamma_{j}.

By Lemma
5.1
, we have an
i
-dimensional subspace
{S}_{i}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\max_{{\bm{x}}\in{S}_{i}}\frac{{\bm{x}}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{N}}_{G}{\bm{x}}}{{\bm{x}}^{\intercal}{\bm{x}}}=\gamma_{i},

from which the lemma follows by the Courant-Fischer theorem (Theorem
2.1
).
∎

Next, similar to the function
\Upsilon(k)

defined in (
4.1
), for any input graph
{G}=({V}_{G},{E}_{G},{w}_{G})
and
(\theta,\ell)
-distinguishable meta-graph
{M}
, we define the function
\Psi(\ell)

by

\Psi(\ell)\triangleq\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\lambda_{\ell+1}}.

Notice that we have by the higher-order Cheeger inequality that
\gamma_{i}/2\leq\rho_{M}(i)
holds for any
i\in[\ell]
, and
\rho_{M}(i)\leq\rho_{G}(k)
by the construction of matrix
{\mathbf{A}}_{M}
. Hence, one can view
\Psi(\ell)
as a refined version of
l/\Upsilon(k)
.

We now see that the vectors
{\bm{f}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{f}}_{\ell}
and
\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{\ell}
are well approximated by each other.
In order to show this, we define for any
i\in[\ell]
the vectors

\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle{\bm{f}}_{j}~\mbox{\ \ \ and\ \ \ }~\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{i},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j},

and can prove the following structure theorem with meta-graphs.

Theorem 5.1

(The Structure Theorem with Meta-Graphs)
.

The following statements hold:

1.

it holds for any
i\in[\ell]
that
\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\leq\gamma_{i}/\lambda_{\ell+1}
;

2.

it holds for any
\ell\in[k]
that

\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\left\|{\bm{f}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\leq\Psi(\ell).

Proof.

For the first statement, we write
\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}
as a linear combination of the vectors
{\bm{f}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{f}}_{n}
, i.e.,

\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle{\bm{f}}_{j}
.
Since
\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}
is a projection of
\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}
, we have that
\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}
is perpendicular to
\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}
, and that

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle^{2}\right)-\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=\ell+1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle^{2}.

Now, we study the quadratic form
\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{N}}_{G}\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}
and have that

\displaystyle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{N}}_{G}\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle{\bm{f}}_{j}^{\intercal}\right){\mathbf{N}}_{G}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle{\bm{f}}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i},{\bm{f}}_{j}\rangle^{2}\lambda_{j}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\ell+1}\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}.

By the proof of Lemma
5.2
, we have that
\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}^{\intercal}{\mathbf{N}}_{G}\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\leq\gamma_{i}
, from which the first statement follows.

Now we prove the second statement.
We define the vectors
\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{k+1},\ldots,\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{n}
to be an arbitrary orthonormal basis of the space orthogonal to the space spanned by

\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{k}
. Then, we can write any
{\bm{f}}_{i}
as

{\bm{f}}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle{\bm{f}}_{i},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}
,
and have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\left\|{\bm{f}}_{i}-\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\left(\left\|{\bm{f}}_{i}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\ell-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{i},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left(1-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\langle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j},{\bm{f}}_{i}\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left(\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left\|\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{j}-\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{j}\right\|^{2}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\frac{\gamma_{j}}{\lambda_{{\ell}+1}},

where the final inequality follows by
the first statement of the theorem.
∎

5.2
Spectral Clustering with Fewer Eigenvectors

In this section, we analyse spectral clustering with fewer eigenvectors. The algorithm is essentially the same as the standard spectral clustering described in Section
4.2
, with the only difference that every
u\in{V}_{G}
is embedded into a point in
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
by the mapping
defined in
(
5.2
).
The analysis follows from the one in Section
4.2
at a very high level.
However, since we require that
\{F(u)\}_{u\in{V}_{G}}
are well separated in
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
for some
\ell<k
, the proof is more involved.
For any
i\in[k]
, we define the approximate centre
{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
of every cluster
{S}_{i}
by

{\bm{p}}^{(i)}(j)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}\cdot\sum_{x=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{j},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{x}\rangle\cdot{\bm{g}}_{x}(i),

and prove that the total
k
-means cost for the points
\{F(u)\}_{u\in{V}_{G}}
can be upper bounded with respect to
\Psi(\ell)
.

Lemma 5.3
.

It holds that

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}{d}(u)\left\|F(u)-{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\leq\Psi(\ell).

Proof.

By definition, it holds that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in S_{i}}{d}(u)\left\|F(u)-{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}{d}(u)\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left(\frac{{\bm{f}}_{j}(u)}{\sqrt{{d}(u)}}-\left(\sum_{x=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{j},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{x}\rangle\frac{{\bm{g}}_{x}(i)}{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left({\bm{f}}_{j}(u)-\left(\sum_{x=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{j},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{x}\rangle\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{x}(u)\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left({\bm{f}}_{j}(u)-\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{j}(u)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left\|{\bm{f}}_{j}-\widehat{{\bm{g}}}_{j}\right\|^{2}\leq\Psi(\ell),

where the final inequality follows from the second statement of Theorem
5.1
.
∎

We now prove a sequence of lemmas establishing that the distance between different
{\bm{p}}^{(i)}
and
{\bm{p}}^{(j)}
can be bounded with respect to
\theta
and
\Psi(\ell)
.

Lemma 5.4
.

It holds for
i\in[k]
that

\left(1-\frac{4\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}{\theta}\right)\frac{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\leq\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\left(1+\frac{2\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}{\theta}\right).

Proof.

It holds by definition that

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})\cdot\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{\ell}\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\sum_{z=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle{\bm{g}}_{y}(i){\bm{g}}_{z}(i)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{\ell}\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle^{2}{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)^{2}+\sum_{x=1}^{\ell}\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z=1\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle{\bm{g}}_{y}(i){\bm{g}}_{z}(i)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{\ell}\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle^{2}{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)^{2}+\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z=1\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}^{\ell}{\bm{g}}_{y}(i){\bm{g}}_{z}(i)\cdot\left(\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}^{\intercal}\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{z}\right).

(5.3)

We study the two terms of (
5.3
) separately. For the second term, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z=1\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}^{\ell}{\bm{g}}_{y}(i){\bm{g}}_{z}(i)\cdot\left(\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}^{\intercal}\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{z}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\lvert{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)\right\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z=1\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}^{\ell}\left\lvert{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)\right\rvert\left\lvert\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}^{\intercal}\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{z}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\lvert{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)\right\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z=1\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}^{\ell}\left\lvert{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)\right\rvert\left\lvert\left(\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}+\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\right)^{\intercal}\left(\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}+\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{z}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\lvert{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)\right\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z=1\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}^{\ell}\left\lvert{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)\right\rvert\left\lvert\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle+\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{z}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle+\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y},\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{z}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\lvert{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)\right\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z=1\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}^{\ell}\left\lvert{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)\right\rvert\left\lvert\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\left(\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)^{2}\right)\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\left\lvert{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)\right\rvert\left\lvert\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle\right\rvert\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\right\|^{2}}\leq\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)},

where we used the fact that
\sum_{y=1}^{k}{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)^{2}=1
for all
i\in[k]
. Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{x=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle^{2}\right){\bm{g}}_{y}(i)^{2}+\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)^{2}+\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}+\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}\leq\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\left(1+\frac{2\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}{\theta}\right).

On the other hand, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{x=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{x},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle^{2}\right){\bm{g}}_{y}(i)^{2}-2\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}\right\|^{2}{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)^{2}-2\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\Psi(\ell)\right)\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}-2\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\left(1-\frac{4\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}{\theta}\right),

where the last inequality holds by the fact that
\theta\leq\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\leq 1
and
\Psi(\ell)<1
. Hence, the statement holds.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

It holds for any different
i,j\in[k]
that

\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}\geq\theta-3\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}.

Proof.

By definition, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{t},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle\left(\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{\ell}\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\langle{\bm{f}}_{t},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle^{2}\left(\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{t=1}^{\ell}\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\langle{\bm{f}}_{t},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle\langle{\bm{f}}_{t},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle\left(\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right)\left(\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right).

We upper bound the second term by

\displaystyle\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\lvert\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\left\lvert\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rvert\sum_{t=1}^{\ell}\left\lvert\langle{\bm{f}}_{t},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\rangle\langle{\bm{f}}_{t},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{x}\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\lvert\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\left\lvert\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rvert\left\lvert\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}^{\intercal}\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{z}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\lvert\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\left\lvert\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rvert\left\lvert\left(\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}+\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\right)^{\intercal}\left(\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}+\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{z}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\lvert\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\left\lvert\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rvert\left\lvert\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left(\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left(\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{y}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right)^{2}\right)\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\left\lvert\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rvert\left\lvert\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle\right\rvert\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(i)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{g}}_{z}(j)}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq z\leq\ell\\
z\neq y\end{subarray}}\langle\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y},\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{z}\rangle^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\sum_{y=1}^{\ell}\left\|\widehat{{\bm{f}}}_{y}-\bar{{\bm{g}}}_{y}\right\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)},

from which we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\Psi(\ell)\right)\theta-2\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}

\displaystyle\geq\theta-3\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}.\qed

Lemma 5.6
.

It holds for any different
i,j\in[k]
that

\left\|\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{\theta}{4}-8\sqrt{\frac{\Psi}{\theta}}.

Proof.

To follow the proof, it may help to refer to the illustration in Figure
5.6
.

{\bm{p}}^{(i)}
{\bm{p}}^{(j)}
\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|}
\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}
{\bm{a}}_{i}
{\bm{a}}_{j}
\frac{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|}{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|}{\bm{a}}_{j}

Figure 5.6
:

Illustration of the proof of Lemma
5.6
.
Our goal is to give a lower bound on the length of
\left(\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right)
, which is the blue dashed line in the figure.
We instead calculate a lower bound on the length of
\left({\bm{a}}_{i}-\frac{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|}{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|}{\bm{a}}_{j}\right)
, which is the red dashed line, and use the fact that by construction
\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|\leq 1
and
\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|\leq 1
.

We set the parameter
\epsilon=4\sqrt{\Psi}/\theta
, and define

{\bm{a}}_{i}=\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}{\left(1+\epsilon\right)\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(i)},\qquad\hskip 30.00005pt{\bm{a}}_{j}=\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}}{\left(1+\epsilon\right)\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(j)}.

By the definition of
\epsilon
and Lemma
5.4
, it holds that

\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|\leq 1
, and
\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|\leq 1
.
We can also assume without loss of generality that
\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|\leq\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|
.
Then, as illustrated in Figure
5.6
, we have

\left\|\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\|^{2}\geq\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}-\frac{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|}{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|}{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|^{2},

and so it suffices to lower bound the right-hand side of the inequality above.
By the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}-\frac{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|}{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|}{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}-{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|-\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}-\frac{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|}{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|}{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|-\left(\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|-\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|\right).

Now, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|-\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}}{(1+\epsilon)\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\cdot\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|-\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}{(1+\epsilon)\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}\cdot\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}=\frac{2\epsilon}{1+\epsilon},

and have by Lemma
5.5
that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}}{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|}\cdot{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\theta-3\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}\geq\sqrt{\theta}-\sqrt{2\epsilon}\geq\sqrt{\theta}-2\epsilon,

since
\epsilon=4\sqrt{\Psi}/\theta<1
by the assumption on
\Psi
.
This gives us

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}-\frac{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{i}\right\|}{\left\|{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|}{\bm{a}}_{j}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sqrt{\theta}-2\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{2\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sqrt{\theta}-4\epsilon\right).

Finally, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|}-\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\left(\sqrt{\theta}-16\frac{\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}{\theta}\right)^{2}\geq\frac{\theta}{4}-8\sqrt{\frac{\Psi(\ell)}{\theta}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

It holds for different
i,j\in[k]
that

\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{\theta^{2}-20\sqrt{\theta\cdot\Psi(\ell)}}{16\min\left\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\right\}}.

Proof.

We assume without loss of generality that
\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\geq\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}
. Then, by Lemma
5.4
and the fact that
\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\|^{2}\geq\theta
holds for any
i\in[k]
,
we have

\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\geq\left(1-\frac{4\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}{\theta}\right)\cdot\frac{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}

and

\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}\geq\left(1-\frac{4\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}{\theta}\right)\cdot\frac{\left\|\bar{{\bm{x}}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})},

which implies that

\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{\theta-4\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}{\min\left\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\right\}}.

Now, we proceed by case distinction.

Case 1:
\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|\geq 4\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|
. In this case, we have

\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|\geq\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|-\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|\geq\frac{3}{4}\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|,

and

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{9}{16}\cdot\frac{\theta-4\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}{\min\left\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\right\}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\theta\left(\theta-20\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)/\theta}\right)}{16\min\left\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\right\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta^{2}-20\sqrt{\theta\cdot\Psi(\ell)}}{16\min\left\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\right\}},

since
\theta<1
.

Case 2:
\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|=\alpha\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|
for some
\alpha\in\left(\frac{1}{4},1\right]
.
By Lemma
5.6
, we have

\left\langle\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|},\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rangle\leq 1-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{4}-8\sqrt{\frac{\Psi}{\theta}}\right)\leq 1-\frac{\theta}{8}+2\sqrt{\frac{\Psi}{\theta}}.

Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}+\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|^{2}-2\left\langle\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(i)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|},\frac{{\bm{p}}^{(j)}}{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|}\right\rangle\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\left(1+\alpha^{2}\right)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}-2\left(1-\frac{\theta}{8}+2\sqrt{\frac{\Psi(\ell)}{\theta}}\right)\alpha\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1+\alpha^{2}-2\alpha+\frac{\theta}{4}\alpha-4\sqrt{\frac{\Psi(\ell)}{\theta}}\alpha\right)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\theta}{4}-4\sqrt{\frac{\Psi(\ell)}{\theta}}\right)\cdot\alpha\cdot\frac{\theta-4\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}}{\min\left\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\right\}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\theta}{16}-\sqrt{\frac{\Psi(\ell)}{\theta}}\right)\left(\theta-4\sqrt{\Psi(\ell)}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\min\left\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\right\}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\theta^{2}}{16}-\frac{5}{4}\sqrt{\theta\Psi(\ell)}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\min\left\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\right\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta^{2}-20\sqrt{\theta\Psi(\ell)}}{16\min\left\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\right\}}

which completes the proof.
∎

It is important to recognise that the lower bound in Lemma
5.7
implies a condition on
\theta
and
\Psi(\ell)
under which
{\bm{p}}^{(i)}
and
{\bm{p}}^{(j)}
are well separated.
With this, we analyse the performance of spectral clustering when fewer eigenvector are employed to construct the embedding and show that it works when the optimal clusters present a noticeable pattern.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
A_{1},\ldots A_{k}
be the output of spectral clustering with
\ell
eigenvectors, and
\sigma
and
{M}_{\sigma,i}
be defined as in (
4.5
) and (
4.6
).
If
\Psi(\ell)\leq\theta^{3}/40^{2}
, then

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\leq 64(1+\textsf{APT})\frac{\Psi(\ell)}{\theta^{2}}.

Proof.

Let us define
{B}_{ij}={A}_{i}\cap{S}_{j}
to be the vertices in
{A}_{i}
which belong to the ground-truth cluster
{S}_{j}
.
Then, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}^{k}\mathrm{vol}({B}_{ij})\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{\sigma(i)})}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({B}_{ij})}{\min\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{\sigma(i)}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\}},

(5.4)

and that

\displaystyle\mathrm{COST}({A}_{1},\ldots{A}_{k})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{A}_{i}}{d}(u)\left\|F(u)-{\bm{c}}_{i}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in{B}_{ij}}{d}(u)\left\|F(u)-{\bm{c}}_{i}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in{B}_{ij}}{d}(u)\left(\frac{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-{\bm{c}}_{i}\right\|^{2}}{2}-\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-F(u)\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\begin{multlined}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in{B}_{ij}}\frac{{d}(u)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-{\bm{p}}^{(\sigma(i))}\right\|^{2}}{8}\qquad\\
-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in{B}_{ij}}{d}(u)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-F(u)\right\|^{2}\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\mathrm{vol}({B}_{ij})\frac{\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(j)}-{\bm{p}}^{(\sigma(i))}\right\|^{2}}{8}-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}{d}(u)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-F(u)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\begin{multlined}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq k\\
j\neq\sigma(i)\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({B}_{ij})}{16\cdot\min\{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{\sigma(i)}),\mathrm{vol}({S}_{j})\}}\left(\theta^{2}-20\sqrt{\theta\cdot\Psi(\ell)}\right)\qquad\\
-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{u\in{S}_{i}}{d}(u)\left\|{\bm{p}}^{(i)}-F(u)\right\|^{2}\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{32}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}\right)\left(\theta^{2}-20\sqrt{\theta\cdot\Psi(\ell)}\right)-\Psi(\ell),

where the second inequality follows by the inequality
\left\|{\bm{a}}-{\bm{b}}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{\left\|{\bm{b}}-{\bm{c}}\right\|^{2}}{2}-\left\|{\bm{a}}-{\bm{c}}\right\|^{2}
, the third inequality follows since
{\bm{c}}_{i}
is closer to
{\bm{p}}^{(\sigma(i))}
than
{\bm{p}}^{(j)}
, the fifth one follows from Lemma
5.7
, and the last one follows by (
5.4
).

On the other hand, since
\mathrm{COST}({A}_{1},\ldots,{A}_{k})\leq\textsf{APT}\cdot\Psi(\ell)
by Lemma
5.3
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\mathrm{vol}({M}_{\sigma,i}\triangle{S}_{i})}{\mathrm{vol}({S}_{i})}

\displaystyle\leq 32\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\Psi(\ell)\cdot\left(\theta^{2}-20\sqrt{\theta\cdot\Psi(\ell)}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 64\cdot(1+\textsf{APT})\cdot\Psi(\ell),

where the last inequality follows by the assumption that
\Psi(\ell)\leq\theta^{3}/1600
. Therefore, the statement follows.
∎

Combining this with other technical ingredients, including the new technique for constructing the desired mapping
\sigma^{*}
described in Section
4.2
, we obtain the performance guarantee of the designed algorithm, which is summarised as follows:

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
{G}=({V},{E})
be a graph with
k
clusters
S_{1},\ldots,S_{k}
of almost balanced size,
and a
(\theta,\ell)
-distinguishable meta-graph that satisfies

\Psi(\ell)\leq\left(2176(1+\textsf{APT})\right)^{-1}\theta^{3}
.
Let
A_{1},\ldots,A_{k}
be the output of spectral clustering with
\ell
eigenvectors, and without loss of generality let the optimal correspondent of
{A}_{i}
be
{S}_{i}
. Then, it holds that

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{vol}\left({A}_{i}\triangle{S}_{i}\right)\leq 2176(1+\textsf{APT})\frac{\Psi(\ell)\cdot\mathrm{vol}({V})}{k\cdot\theta^{2}}.

Proof.

This result can be obtained by using the same technique as the one used in the proof of Theorem
4.2
, but applying Lemma
5.3
instead of Lemma
4.1
and
Lemma
5.8
instead of Lemma
4.6
in the analysis.
∎

Notice that if we take
\ell=k
, then we have that
\theta=1
and
\Psi(\ell)\leq k/\Upsilon(k)
which makes the guarantee in Theorem
5.2
the same as the one in Theorem
4.2
.
However, if the meta-graph corresponding to the optimal clusters is
(\theta,\ell)
-distinguishable for large
\theta
and
\ell\ll k
, then it holds that
\Psi(\ell)\ll k/\Upsilon(k)
and Theorem
5.2
gives a stronger guarantee than the
one from Theorem
4.2
.

5.3
Experimental Results

In this section we empirically evaluate the performance of spectral clustering for finding
k
clusters while using fewer than
k
eigenvectors.
Our results on synthetic data demonstrate that, for graphs with a clear pattern of clusters,
spectral clustering with fewer than
k
eigenvectors performs better.
This is further confirmed on real-world datasets including BSDS, MNIST, and USPS.
The code used to produce all experimental results is available at

https://github.com/pmacg/spectral-clustering-meta-graphs
.

We implement spectral clustering in Python, using the
scipy
library for computing eigenvectors, and the
k
-means algorithm from the
sklearn
library.
Our experiments on synthetic data are performed on a desktop computer with an Intel(R) Core(TM) i5-8500 CPU @ 3.00GHz processor and 16 GB RAM.
The experiments on the BSDS, MNIST, and USPS datasets are performed on a compute server with 64 AMD EPYC 7302 16-Core Processors.

5.3.1
Results on Synthetic Data

We first study the performance of spectral clustering on random graphs whose clusters exhibit a clear pattern.
Given the parameters
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
,
0\leq q\leq p\leq 1
, and some meta-graph
{M}=({V}_{M},{E}_{M})
with
k
vertices, we generate a graph with clusters
\{{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
, each of size
n
, as follows.
For each pair of vertices
u\in{S}_{i}
and
v\in{S}_{j}
, we add the edge
(u,v)
with probability
p
if
i=j
, and with probability
q
if
i\neq j

and
(i,j)\in{E}_{M}
.
The metric used for our evaluation is defined by

\frac{1}{nk}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left\lvert{S}_{i}\cap{A}_{i}\right\rvert
,
for the optimal matching between the output
\{{A}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
and the ground truth
\{{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
.

In our experiments, we fix
n=1000
,
p=0.01
, and consider the meta-graphs
C_{10}
and
P_{4,4}
, similar to those illustrated in Examples
5.1
and
5.2
; this results in graphs with 10,000 and 16,000 vertices respectively.
We vary the ratio
p/q
and the number of eigenvectors used to find the clusters.
Our experimental result, which is reported as the average score over 10 trials and shown in
Figure
5.7
, clearly shows that spectral clustering with fewer than
k
eigenvectors performs better. This is particularly the case when
p
and
q
are close, which corresponds to the more challenging regime in the model.

(a)

Meta-Graph
C_{10}

(b)

Meta-Graph
P_{4,4}

Figure 5.7
:

A comparison on the output of spectral clustering with meta-graphs
C_{10}
and
P_{4,4}
, when different number of eigenvectors are used.
Note that classical spectral clustering uses
10
and
16
eigenvectors respectively.

5.3.2
Results on the BSDS Dataset

In this experiment, we study the performance of spectral clustering for image segmentation when using different numbers of eigenvectors.
We consider the Berkeley Segmentation Data Set (BSDS)
[
AMFM11
]
, which consists of
500
images along with their ground-truth segmentations.
For each image, we construct a similarity graph on the pixels and take
k
to be the number of clusters in the ground-truth segmentation
3
3

3

The BSDS dataset provides several human-generated ground truth segmentations for each image.
Since there are different numbers of ground truth clusterings associated with each image, in our experiments we take the target number of clusters for a given image to be the
one closest to the median.

.
Given a particular image in the dataset, we first downsample the image to have at most 20,000 pixels.
Then, we represent each pixel by the point
(r,g,b,x,y)^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{5}
, where
r,g,b\in[255]
are the RGB values of the pixel and
x
and
y
are the coordinates of the pixel in the downsampled image.
We construct the similarity graph by taking each pixel to be a vertex in the graph, and for every pair of pixels
{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{R}^{5}
, we add an edge with weight
\exp(-\left\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}}\right\|^{2}/2\sigma^{2})
where
\sigma=20
.
Then we apply spectral clustering, varying the number of eigenvectors used.
We evaluate each segmentation produced with spectral clustering using the Rand Index
[
Ran71
]
as implemented in the benchmarking code provided along with the BSDS dataset.
For each image, this computes the average Rand Index across all of the provided ground-truth segmentations for the image.
Figure
5.3
shows two images from the dataset along with the segmentations produced with spectral clustering, and additional examples are given in Figures
5.9
and
5.10
.
These examples demonstrate that spectral clustering with fewer eigenvectors performs better.

We conduct the experiments on the entire BSDS dataset, and the average Rand Index of the algorithm’s output is reported in Table
5.1
: it is clear that spectral clustering with
k/2
eigenvectors consistently out-performs the one with
k
eigenvectors. We further notice that, on
89\%
of the images across the entire dataset, using fewer than
k
eigenvectors gives a better result than using
k
eigenvectors.

Table 5.1
:
The average Rand Index across the BSDS dataset for different numbers of eigenvectors.

Optimal

refers to the algorithm which runs spectral clustering with
\ell
eigenvectors for all possible
\ell\in[k]
and returns the output with the highest Rand Index.

Average Rand Index

5.3.3
Results on the MNIST and USPS Datasets

We further demonstrate the real-world applicability of our results on the MNIST and USPS datasets
[
Hul94
,
LBBH98
]
, which consist of images of hand-written digits.
In both the MNIST and USPS datasets, each image is represented as an array of greyscale pixels with values between 0 and 255.
The MNIST dataset has 60,000 images with dimensions
28\times 28
and the USPS dataset has 7,291 images with dimensions
16\times 16
.
In each dataset, we consider each image to be a single data point in
\mathbb{R}^{(d^{2})}
where
d
is the dimension of the images and construct the
k
nearest neighbour graph for
k=3
.
For the MNIST dataset, this gives a graph with 60,000 vertices and 138,563 edges, and for the USPS dataset this gives a graph with 7,291 vertices and 16,715 edges.
We use spectral clustering to partition the graphs into
10
clusters.
We measure the similarity between the found clusters and the ground truth using the Adjusted Rand Index
[
GA17
]
and accuracy
[
Ran71
]
,
and plot the results in Figure
5.8
.
Our experiments show that spectral clustering with just 7 eigenvectors gives the best performance on both datasets.

Experimental results on the MNIST and USPS datasets.
